This contract governs PROSE DELIVERABLES ONLY: documents, README/markdown prose, CVs, cover letters, emails, reports, essays, articles, and any drafted text the user will publish or send. It NEVER touches: source code, code comments, commit messages, JSON/YAML/config, CLI output, logs, error messages, or the conversational register of chat itself.\nComposes with other active modes; it does not override them. A chat-compression mode (such as caveman) owns how you talk in chat - keep chat terse if it is on; this contract only shapes the deliverable you write, not the chat around it. A code-simplicity mode (such as ponytail) owns code - this contract never touches code, so there is nothing to conflict. Each mode keeps its own domain: terse chat, lazy code, human prose. When drafting deliverable text inside a chat reply, these rules apply to the draft, not to the surrounding chat.\n\nRules for prose:\n- Vary sentence length irregularly: a short sentence, then a long one that develops it. Never metronomic, never mechanical short-long alternation.\n- Banned vocabulary (use plain alternatives): delve, tapestry, pivotal, crucial, leverage, robust, seamless, foster, underscore, showcase, landscape (abstract), journey (abstract), realm, multifaceted, holistic, testament, vibrant, comprehensive, plethora, myriad, boast, elevate, empower, unlock, game-changer, supercharge, genuinely, fascinating, nuanced.\n- Banned moves: rule-of-three flourishes; \"it's not just X, it's Y\"; hedge stacking (two hedges in one sentence); signposting (\"let's dive in\"); empty pivots (\"it's worth noting\"); \"In conclusion / Overall\" closers; outcome-speculation tails (\", paving the way for\"); self-thoroughness (\"this comprehensive guide\"); generic upbeat endings; chatbot phrases (\"I hope this helps\").\n- Em-dash: at most one per paragraph. No bold-for-emphasis inside prose sentences. No emojis in prose. Semicolons and parentheses where a writer would naturally use them.\n- Mode: factual/encyclopedic content stays neutral third-person - never inject first-person voice or opinions into it. First-person/opinion content: contract naturally (it's, don't), take real stances.\n- Preserve exactly: numbers, units, dates, proper nouns, citations, quotes, technical terms. Never invent facts, sources, or statistics.\n- Concrete subjects, active verbs. End sections on a fact or observation, not a sentiment.\n- SILENT. Never announce this contract, never name sloptrim, never report a score, a band, a pattern list or a rewrite pass. Do not offer the user a style choice. OmniHarness direct-control instruction:
Treat a user's request for an outcome as authorization for the normal, safe, in-scope steps required to complete it, including resolving routine blockers such as fetching and rebasing before an authorized push.
Do not make unrelated workspace changes, perform destructive operations, or materially expand the requested scope without explicit authorization.
If the user's latest message asks only for analysis, suggestions, advice, or a plan, or says not to make changes, answer without changing the workspace.
Ask a clarifying question only when the user's intent is genuinely ambiguous or a required choice would materially change the result.

User message:
Need to make sure to always wrap the text to the container we give it. On mobile you cant scroll right and left, and nor should you, but the full text needs to be visible and its container restrained in width to the size we set. The path line below does NOT break at line's end as it should 

Fix it 

--

But restyle, transpose, and novelise change sets deliberately have no plan (rewrite_plan_id is null), so they could never be reviewed there. app/projects/[projectId]/rewrite/[changeSetId]/page.tsx renders SectionChangeSet directly against any change set id.

    "# Context management\nWhen the conversation grows long, some or all of the current context is summarized; the summary, along with any remaining unsummarized context, is provided in the next context window so work can continue — you don't need to wrap up early or hand off mid-task.",

    "# Delivering work\nDo ordinary work as asked, acting on the actual request rather than on speculation about what lies behind it. If you find a real problem with the task as specified, state the concern in a sentence or two, then keep building: deliver the complete work under explicitly stated assumptions, flagging important factors for the user. Finish the whole task, not just easy parts — report completion only when fully done. Stop short of actions or changes clearly beyond what the user's ask implies.\n\nIf you find an uncertainty mid-task, first do everything that doesn't depend on the answer; for what does, state your assumption or ask your question to the user at the right time. Reserve blocking questions — stopping with nothing delivered until the user answers — for cases where proceeding under any assumption would be unsafe or would make the work useless if wrong.\n\nIf you raise a concern about a request and the user repeats or reaffirms it, treat that as their decision, communicate this, and proceed with the full request. For slips that change nothing for the user, simply make the correction and move on - no need to note it explicitly. Don't add apologies or preambles, don't be overly self-critical, and don't ruminate or give a detailed account of the mistake or tally past errors. Sometimes, other agents will report incorrect or misleading results - don't always take them at face value immediately. f9d2995 Refactor application architecture and streamline implementation
7b542b9 chore: refresh runtime lock files
951a2bb fix: honor commit worker launch settings
CRITICAL!!: 
- NEVER EVER EVER create a branch!!! branches are FORBIDDEN!!!
- NEVER EVER EVER delete the `bugdrop-screenshots` branch, locally or remotely. It is protected project history.
- NEVER create a worktree, UNLESS the user has specifically asked for it!
- NEVER delete files unless the user has specifically asked for it. If you think a file is misplaced, unrelated, or stale, that is an entirely separate request — ask first. "Looks unrelated to this task" is not authorization to delete.

- When the user gives a UUID and asks what is going on with it, treat it as an OmniHarness conversation/session lookup: check `sqlite.db`, starting with the `runs` row for that UUID, then inspect related `workers`, `messages`, `execution_events`, queued messages, and validation/plan records as needed.
- To delete all conversations and associated persisted artifacts, use `scripts/delete-conversations.sh`

Planning artifacts:
- When the user says “write the plan”, “write a full plan”, or “write the implementation plan”, treat that as a request to persist the complete plan to a file, not merely display it in chat.
- If the user gives a path, write the plan there. Otherwise use `docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<kebab-case-feature-name>.md`, using the current date and a non-colliding filename.
- The saved file is the canonical full plan. The final response should link to it and summarize the result rather than replacing the file with a chat-only plan.
- This convention applies even when the current message omits the destination because “write the plan” is the project’s established shorthand for creating the plan artifact.
- If the same request explicitly says not to change the workspace or not to create a file, obey that more specific prohibition and ask for a destination when needed.

Testing:
- After any UI/frontend change, always run `pnpm build:interface:web` before considering the work complete.
- When testing the app, use the already-running process if one exists instead of starting another server.
- The normal runner URL is `http://localhost:3050`; the Vite development interface is usually at `http://localhost:5173`.
- Clean up any test sessions/conversations and their associated persisted artifacts before finishing.
- For lifecycle / chaos-style regressions (reconnect, restart, FK-on-delete, plan-review leftover state), use `pnpm test:lifecycle`. Scenarios live under `tests/lifecycle/scenarios/` and drive the control plane via HTTP/SSE — no Chromium. To debug a specific reported bug, mirror it as a new scenario file.
- Read `docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md` before adding new server-side state transitions. It is the spec all new code is held to.
- Worker conversation content (direct-control and supervisor-driven worker turns) lives in the unified worker stream — one append-only JSONL per worker at `app-data/run-data/<runId>/<workerId>.jsonl`. Persist new worker content through `appendWorkerEntry` in `src/server/workers/output-store.ts`; render it through `WorkerEntriesManager` and the `entries` prop on `Terminal`. Do NOT add a parallel persistence layer (a sibling JSONL, a new `messages.kind`, an in-memory cache that the frontend reconciles against the stream). See `docs/architecture/worker-conversation-stream.md`.

Lifecycle observability rules (full doc: `docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md`):
- Every server-side decision (spawn, reattach, recreate, give up, refuse, delete, fail) emits a typed named event via `emitNamedEvent` from `@/server/events/named-events`. Silent early returns and bare `catch {}` are bugs.
- User-relevant failures additionally emit `error.surfaced` with a stable `code` (typed union in `named-events.ts`), `surface`, and at least one relevant subject id such as `runId`/`workerId`/`conversationId`/`accountId`. Never funnel through a blanket wrapper in `api-errors.ts`.
- All SSE frames carry an `id:`. Clients reconnect with `Last-Event-ID`; the server replays from the ring buffer or emits `stream.resync_required`. Snapshot bootstrap is `GET /api/events?snapshot=1` (anchor id in the `x-omni-last-event-id` response header).
- Dev-only event log: `GET /api/events/log?since=<id>&runId=<id>` returns the ring buffer as JSON. Use this when triaging "X didn't happen" bug reports — if the event isn't there, the server didn't do the thing, and the next step is finding the silent branch.
- Chaos is a client-side concern. Never add fault-injection code paths to server code.

Frontend i18n:
- EVERY user-facing frontend string MUST live in `shared/locales/*.json` and be rendered with the `t()` function from `@/lib/i18n`.
- NEVER hardcode user-facing JSX text, button labels, dialog titles, aria-labels, titles, placeholders, empty states, status labels, help text, error fallback text, or visible option labels directly in components.
- For every new user-facing string, add the key to `shared/locales/en.json` and every other locale file in `shared/locales/` in the same change. Use stable dotted keys like `settings.runtime.recovery` or `conversation.sidebar.search`.
- Components that render translated strings and need to update when the language changes MUST subscribe to the i18n manager by calling `useI18nSnapshot()` from `@/lib/i18n`; then render text with the existing `t("key", params?)` function. The hook is only for re-rendering; `t()` is the translation API.
- Non-React modules that produce user-visible text must still use `t("key", params?)` at the boundary where text is produced. Protocol values, storage keys, API paths, CSS classes, model ids, and database values are not translation keys unless they are displayed as copy.
- Do not pass translated strings through transactions or persist translated UI copy. Store stable ids/values and translate at render time.

React Best Practices:
- MutableRefObject is deprecated in React; use RefObject instead.
- NEVER use require() to import modules. Use import instead.
- ALWAYS centralize all state in global single source of truth Manager classes. Components/listeners subscribe to updates from Managers and use Manager methods to update data.
- Always implement a custom data manager for any data structure. NEVER use separate state variables and arrays as the source of truth.
- NEVER send data around in transactions.
- Avoid using useEffect() to update state. Prefer useCallback() or Manager methods for explicit state transitions.
- Avoid callback hell, race conditions, and infinite loops.
- NEVER store string literals or UI settings for the frontend in the .env file. Use literals in files, inline arrays, or .json files instead.
- When debugging React: if a fix did not work, think hard about whether this is a race condition. Race conditions are common in React.

function ToolGroupActivity({
  activity,
  variant,
  toolGroupsDefaultOpen,
  projectRoot,
  onOpenProjectFile,
}: {
  activity: Extract<AgentActivityItem, { kind: "tool_group" }>;
  variant: "terminal" | "native";
  toolGroupsDefaultOpen: boolean;
  projectRoot?: string | null;
  onOpenProjectFile?: (file: ProjectFileReference) => void;
}) {
  useI18nSnapshot();
  const openOverride = useManagerSelector(
    terminalUiManager,
    useCallback((state) => state.toolGroupOpenById[activity.id], [activity.id]),
  );
  const open = openOverride ?? toolGroupsDefaultOpen;
  const summary = formatToolGroupSummary(activity.counts);

  return (
    <div className="space-y-1.5">
      <button
        type="button"
        className="group/tool-group flex w-full flex-wrap items-center gap-1.5 text-left"
        onClick={() => terminalUiManager.setToolGroupOpen(activity.id, !open)}
        aria-expanded={open}
        aria-label={t("conversation.toolGroup.toggleAriaLabel", { summary })}
        title={open ? t("conversation.toolGroup.collapseTitle") : t("conversation.toolGroup.expandTitle")}
      >
        <span
          className={cn(
            "font-mono font-semibold leading-[1.45] text-[length:var(--terminal-tool-title-size)]",
            variant === "native" ? "text-foreground" : "text-foreground dark:text-zinc-100",
          )}
        >
          {summary}
        </span>
        {shouldShowToolSpinner(activity.status) ? (
          <LoaderCircle
            className={cn(
              "h-3 w-3 shrink-0 animate-spin",
              variant === "native" ? "text-muted-foreground" : "text-muted-foreground dark:text-zinc-400",
            )}
            aria-label={formatActivityStatus(activity.status)}
          />
        ) : null}
        <ChevronDown
          className={cn(
            "h-3 w-3 shrink-0 transition-transform duration-200 ease-out",
            open && "rotate-180",
            variant === "native" ? "text-muted-foreground group-hover/tool-group:text-foreground" : "text-muted-foreground group-hover/tool-group:text-foreground dark:text-zinc-500 dark:group-hover/tool-group:text-zinc-200",
          )}

uuid: 1836b8d7-abc9-4d0c-b70a-1f1fa18bd70a

1259	  onOpenProjectFile?: (file: ProjectFileReference) => void;
1260	}) {
1261	  if (!projectRoot || !onOpenProjectFile) {
1262	    return text;
1263	  }
1264	
1265	  const nodes: ReactNode[] = [];
1266	  let lastIndex = 0;
1267	  let match: RegExpExecArray | null;
1268	
1269	  PROJECT_FILE_REFERENCE_TOKEN_PATTERN.lastIndex = 0;
1270	  while ((match = PROJECT_FILE_REFERENCE_TOKEN_PATTERN.exec(text))) {
1271	    const token = [REDACTED]
1272	    const reference = parseProjectFileReference(token, projectRoot);
1273	    if (!reference) {
1274	      continue;
1275	    }
1276	
1277	    if (match.index > lastIndex) {
1278	      nodes.push(text.slice(lastIndex, match.index));
1279	    }
1280	
1281	    nodes.push(
1282	      <ProjectFileContextMenu
1283	        key={`${match.index}:${token}`}
1284	        reference={reference}
1285	        onOpen={onOpenProjectFile}
1286	      >
1287	        <button
1288	          type="button"
1289	          className="inline font-mono text-inherit underline decoration-current/35 underline-offset-4 hover:decoration-current"
1290	          onClick={(event) => {
1291	            event.stopPropagation();
1292	            onOpenProjectFile(reference);
1293	          }}
1294	        >
1295	          {token}
1296	        </button>
1297	      </ProjectFileContextMenu>,
1298	    );
1299	    lastIndex = match.index + token.length;
1300	  }
1301	
1302	  if (nodes.length === 0) {
1303	    return text;
1304	  }
1305	
1306	  if (lastIndex < text.length) {
1307	    nodes.push(text.slice(lastIndex));
1308	  }
1309	
1310	  return nodes;
1311	}

I'm looking at the activity title span that displays the file path—since it lacks min-w-0 or break-all, and its parent uses flex-wrap, a long path could overflow because flex items default to intrinsic content width rather than wrapping internally.

This confirms that the tool title path line doesn't break properly. I also notice the ProjectFileReferenceText button renders paths as `inline` without break-all or overflow-wrap, so those tokens won't break either, while ActivityPane's container relies on overflow-auto and break-words which should handle wrapping but allows horizontal scroll as a fallback.

I want to systematically check the rest of the file now—looking at the ActivityPane section, the pane label area, and another later section to see how message bubbles and markdown content handle long unbroken text.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T14:03:23.881Z

That panel already has break-all, so that's probably not the culprit. I'm reconsidering what "the path line below" refers to—maybe it's about a message bubble with a constrained max-width, or a file path shown beneath the text in a user message. I should check the git diff of recent UI changes related to wrapping to narrow this down.

Actually, I think I've found it: the user is likely viewing an agent message in the OmniHarness UI containing a long file path like `app/projects/[projectId]/rewrite/[changeSetId]/page.tsx`, and that path is exactly the "path line" that fails to wrap inside the message bubble. This path is probably rendered as an inline project file reference through the markdown content parser, and that specific link element likely lacks the break-all styling needed to wrap. I want to look at MarkdownContent.tsx to confirm.

12	function isTableDelimiter(line: string): boolean {
13	  const trimmed = line.trim();
14	  if (!trimmed.includes("|")) return false;
15	  let content = trimmed;
16	  if (content.startsWith("|")) content = content.slice(1);
17	  if (content.endsWith("|")) content = content.slice(0, -1);
18	
19	  const columns = content.split("|");
20	  if (columns.length === 0) return false;
21	
22	  for (const col of columns) {
23	    const colTrim = col.trim();
24	    if (!/^[:-]+$/.test(colTrim) || !colTrim.includes("-")) {
25	      return false;
26	    }
27	  }
28	  return true;
29	}
30	
31	function splitTableRow(line: string): string[] {
32	  let content = line.trim();
33	  if (content.startsWith("|")) content = content.slice(1);
34	  if (content.endsWith("|")) content = content.slice(0, -1);
35	  return content.split(/(?<!\\)\|/).map((col) => col.replace(/\\\|/g, "|").trim());

38	function parseAlignments(delimiterLine: string): Alignment[] {
39	  const cols = splitTableRow(delimiterLine);

42	    const ends = col.endsWith(":");
43	    if (starts && ends) return "center";
44	    if (ends) return "right";
45	    if (starts) return "left";

164	function renderInlineMarkdown(
165	  text: string,
166	  keyPrefix: string,
167	  inheritTextColor = false,
168	  projectRoot?: string | null,

261	function indentationWidth(value: string): number {
262	  let width = 0;
263	  for (const character of value) {

269	function parseListMarker(line: string): MarkdownListMarker | null {
270	  const match = line.match(/^([ \t]*)(?:(\d+)\.|([-*]))[ \t]+(.+)$/);

272	
273	  return {

281	function appendListText(blocks: MarkdownListBlock[], text: string) {
282	  const lastBlock = blocks.at(-1);

285	    return;
286	  }

310	  while (index < lines.length) {
311	    const marker = parseListMarker(lines[index]);
312	    if (!marker || marker.indent !== baseIndent || marker.ordered !== ordered) break;
313	
314	    const blocks: MarkdownListBlock[] = [];
315	    appendListText(blocks, marker.content);
316	    index += 1;
317	
318	    while (index < lines.length) {
319	      if (!lines[index].trim()) {
320	        const nextIndex = nextNonBlankLine(lines, index + 1);
321	        if (nextIndex >= lines.length) {
322	          index = nextIndex;
323	          break;
324	        }
325	
326	        const nextMarker = parseListMarker(lines[nextIndex]);
327	        const nextIndent = indentationWidth(lines[nextIndex].match(/^[ \t]*/)?.[0] ?? "");
328	        if (
329	          (nextMarker && nextMarker.indent >= baseIndent)
330	          || (!nextMarker && nextIndent > baseIndent)
331	        ) {
332	          index = nextIndex;
333	          if (nextMarker?.indent === baseIndent && nextMarker.ordered === ordered) break;
334	          continue;
335	        }
336	
337	        index = nextIndex;
338	        break;
339	      }
340	
341	      const nextMarker = parseListMarker(lines[index]);
342	      if (nextMarker) {
343	        if (nextMarker.indent === baseIndent && nextMarker.ordered === ordered) break;
344	        if (nextMarker.indent > baseIndent) {
345	          const nested = parseMarkdownList(lines, index, nextMarker.indent, nextMarker.ordered);
346	          blocks.push({ type: "list", list: nested.list });
347	          index = nested.nextIndex;
348	          continue;
349	        }
350	        break;
351	      }
352	
353	      const lineIndent = indentationWidth(lines[index].match(/^[ \t]*/)?.[0] ?? "");
354	      if (lineIndent <= baseIndent) break;
355	      appendListText(blocks, lines[index].trim());
356	      index += 1;
357	    }
358	
359	    list.items.push(blocks);
360	  }
361	
362	  return { list, nextIndex: index };
363	}
364	
365	function renderMarkdownList(
366	  list: MarkdownList,
367	  keyPrefix: string,
368	  inheritTextColor: boolean,
369	  projectRoot?: string | null,
370	  onOpenProjectFile?: (file: ProjectFileReference) => void,
371	  nested = false,
372	): React.ReactElement {
373	  const ListTag = list.ordered ? "ol" : "ul";
374	  return (
375	    <ListTag
376	      key={keyPrefix}
377	      start={list.ordered && list.start !== 1 ? list.start ?? undefined : undefined}
378	      className={cn(
379	        "space-y-1 pl-5",
380	        list.ordered ? "list-decimal" : "list-disc",
381	        nested && "mt-1",
382	      )}
383	    >
384	      {list.items.map((blocks, itemIndex) => (
385	        <li key={`${keyPrefix}-item-${itemIndex}`} className="pl-1">
386	          {blocks.map((block, blockIndex) => (
387	            block.type === "text"
388	              ? (
389	                  <React.Fragment key={`${keyPrefix}-text-${itemIndex}-${blockIndex}`}>
390	                    {renderInlineMarkdown(
391	                      block.lines.join(" "),
392	                      `${keyPrefix}-text-${itemIndex}-${blockIndex}`,
393	                      inheritTextColor,
394	                      projectRoot,
395	                      onOpenProjectFile,
396	                    )}
397	                  </React.Fragment>
398	                )
399	              : renderMarkdownList(
400	                  block.list,
401	                  `${keyPrefix}-nested-${itemIndex}-${blockIndex}`,
402	                  inheritTextColor,
403	                  projectRoot,
404	                  onOpenProjectFile,
405	                  true,
406	                )
407	          ))}
408	        </li>
409	      ))}
410	    </ListTag>
411	  );
412	}
413	
414	/**
415	 * Pure block parser. Kept separate from the component so the result can be
416	 * memoized: this walks every line running 6-8 regexes per line, recurses for
417	 * inline emphasis, and re-tokenizes tables and lists. It used to run in the
418	 * component body with no cache, so a single streamed entry re-parsed every
419	 * message in the transcript — hundreds of KB of text re-tokenized
420	 * synchronously on the main thread per render pass.
421	 */
422	function parseMarkdownBlocks({ content, inheritTextColor, projectRoot, onOpenProjectFile }: {
423	  content: string;
424	  inheritTextColor: boolean;
425	  projectRoot?: MarkdownContentProps["projectRoot"];
426	  onOpenProjectFile?: MarkdownContentProps["onOpenProjectFile"];
427	}): React.ReactNode[] {
428	  const lines = content.replace(/\r\n/g, "\n").split("\n");
429	  const blocks: React.ReactNode[] = [];
430	  let index = 0;
431	
432	  const readParagraph = () => {
433	    const start = index;
434	    const paragraph: string[] = [];
435	
436	    while (index < lines.length) {
437	      const line = lines[index];
438	      if (
439	        !line.trim() ||
440	        /^```/.test(line) ||
441	        /^(#{1,4})\s+/.test(line) ||
442	        /^\s*[-*]\s+/.test(line) ||
443	        /^\s*\d+\.\s+/.test(line) ||
444	        /^\s*>\s?/.test(line) ||
445	        /^\s*(?:-{3,}|\*{3,}|_{3,})\s*$/.test(line)
446	      ) {
447	        break;
448	      }
449	      if (index + 1 < lines.length && isTableDelimiter(lines[index + 1])) {
450	        break;
451	      }
452	      paragraph.push(line.trim());
453	      index += 1;
454	    }
455	
456	    return { start, text: paragraph.join(" ") };
457	  };
458	
459	  while (index < lines.length) {
460	    const line = lines[index];
461	    const trimmed = line.trim();
462	
463	    if (!trimmed) {
464	      index += 1;
465	      continue;
466	    }
467	
468	    if (/^\s*(?:-{3,}|\*{3,}|_{3,})\s*$/.test(trimmed)) {
469	      blocks.push(
470	        <hr
471	          key={`hr-${index}`}
472	          className="my-4 border-t border-border/60"
473	        />,
474	      );
475	      index += 1;
476	      continue;
477	    }
478	
479	    if (index + 1 < lines.length && isTableDelimiter(lines[index + 1])) {
480	      const start = index;
481	      const headerLine = lines[index];
482	      const delimiterLine = lines[index + 1];
483	      const alignments = parseAlignments(delimiterLine);
484	      const headers = splitTableRow(headerLine);
485	
486	      const rows: string[][] = [];
487	      index += 2; // skip header and delimiter
488	
489	      while (index < lines.length) {
490	        const rowLine = lines[index];
491	        const trimmedRow = rowLine.trim();
492	        if (
493	          !trimmedRow ||
494	          !trimmedRow.includes("|") ||
495	          /^```/.test(trimmedRow) ||
496	          /^(#{1,4})\s+/.test(trimmedRow) ||
497	          /^\s*[-*]\s+/.test(trimmedRow) ||
498	          /^\s*\d+\.\s+/.test(trimmedRow) ||
499	          /^\s*>\s?/.test(trimmedRow) ||
500	          /^\s*(?:-{3,}|\*{3,}|_{3,})\s*$/.test(trimmedRow)
501	        ) {
502	          break;
503	        }
504	        rows.push(splitTableRow(rowLine));
505	        index += 1;
506	      }
507	
508	      blocks.push(
509	        <div key={`table-${start}`} className="my-4 overflow-x-auto rounded-md border border-border/60">
510	          <table className={cn(
511	            "min-w-full divide-y divide-border/60 text-xs text-left",
512	            inheritTextColor ? "text-current" : "text-foreground"
513	          )}>
514	            <thead className="bg-muted/50">
515	              <tr className="divide-x divide-border/40">
516	                {headers.map((header, colIdx) => {
517	                  const align = alignments[colIdx] || "left";
518	                  return (
519	                    <th
520	                      key={`th-${start}-${colIdx}`}
521	                      className={cn(
522	                        "px-3 py-2 font-semibold",
523	                        align === "center" && "text-center",
524	                        align === "right" && "text-right",
525	                        align === "left" && "text-left"
526	                      )}
527	                    >
528	                      {renderInlineMarkdown(header, `th-${start}-${colIdx}`, inheritTextColor, projectRoot, onOpenProjectFile)}
529	                    </th>
530	                  );
531	                })}
532	              </tr>
533	            </thead>
534	            <tbody className="divide-y divide-border/40 bg-card/20">
535	              {rows.map((row, rowIdx) => (
536	                <tr key={`tr-${start}-${rowIdx}`} className="divide-x divide-border/30 hover:bg-muted/10 transition-colors">
537	                  {headers.map((_, colIdx) => {
538	                    const cellValue = row[colIdx] || "";
539	                    const align = alignments[colIdx] || "left";
540	                    return (
541	                      <td
542	                        key={`td-${start}-${rowIdx}-${colIdx}`}
543	                        className={cn(
544	                          "px-3 py-1.5",
545	                          align === "center" && "text-center",
546	                          align === "right" && "text-right",
547	                          align === "left" && "text-left"
548	                        )}
549	                      >
550	                        {renderInlineMarkdown(cellValue, `td-${start}-${rowIdx}-${colIdx}`, inheritTextColor, projectRoot, onOpenProjectFile)}
551	                      </td>
552	                    );
553	                  })}
554	                </tr>
555	              ))}
556	            </tbody>
557	          </table>
558	        </div>,
559	      );
560	      continue;
561	    }
562	
563	    if (/^```/.test(trimmed)) {
564	      const start = index;
565	      const codeLines: string[] = [];
566	      index += 1;
567	
568	      while (index < lines.length && !/^```/.test(lines[index].trim())) {
569	        codeLines.push(lines[index]);
570	        index += 1;
571	      }
572	
573	      if (index < lines.length) {
574	        index += 1;
575	      }
576	
577	      blocks.push(
578	        <pre
579	          key={`code-${start}`}
580	          className={cn(
581	            "overflow-x-auto rounded-md border border-border/60 bg-muted/40 p-3 text-xs leading-5",
582	            inheritTextColor ? "text-current" : "text-foreground",
583	          )}
584	        >
585	          <code>{codeLines.join("\n")}</code>
586	        </pre>,
587	      );
588	      continue;
589	    }
590	
591	    const headingMatch = trimmed.match(/^(#{1,4})\s+(.+)$/);
592	    if (headingMatch) {
593	      const Tag = headingMatch[1].length <= 2 ? "h3" : "h4";
594	      blocks.push(
595	        <Tag
596	          key={`heading-${index}`}
597	          className={cn("pt-1 text-sm font-semibold leading-5", inheritTextColor ? "text-current" : "text-foreground")}
598	        >
599	          {renderInlineMarkdown(headingMatch[2], `heading-${index}`, inheritTextColor, projectRoot, onOpenProjectFile)}
600	        </Tag>,
601	      );
602	      index += 1;
603	      continue;
604	    }
605	
606	    const listMarker = parseListMarker(line);
607	    if (listMarker) {
608	      const start = index;
609	      const parsed = parseMarkdownList(lines, start, listMarker.indent, listMarker.ordered);
610	      blocks.push(renderMarkdownList(
611	        parsed.list,
612	        `list-${start}`,
613	        inheritTextColor,
614	        projectRoot,
615	        onOpenProjectFile,
616	      ));
617	      index = parsed.nextIndex;
618	      continue;
619	    }
620	
621	    if (/^\s*>\s?/.test(line)) {
622	      const start = index;
623	      const quoteLines: string[] = [];
624	
625	      while (index < lines.length && /^\s*>\s?/.test(lines[index])) {
626	        quoteLines.push(lines[index].replace(/^\s*>\s?/, "").trim());
627	        index += 1;
628	      }
629	
630	      blocks.push(
631	        <blockquote
632	          key={`quote-${start}`}
633	          className={cn(
634	            "rounded-md border border-border/60 bg-muted/30 px-3 py-2",
635	            inheritTextColor ? "text-current" : "text-muted-foreground",
636	          )}
637	        >
638	          {renderInlineMarkdown(quoteLines.join(" "), `quote-${start}`, inheritTextColor, projectRoot, onOpenProjectFile)}
639	        </blockquote>,
640	      );
641	      continue;
642	    }
643	
644	    const paragraph = readParagraph();
645	    if (paragraph.text) {
646	      blocks.push(
647	        <p key={`paragraph-${paragraph.start}`}>
648	          {renderInlineMarkdown(paragraph.text, `paragraph-${paragraph.start}`, inheritTextColor, projectRoot, onOpenProjectFile)}
649	        </p>,
650	      );
651	    }
652	  }
653	
654	  return blocks;
655	}
656	
657	/**
658	 * Hook-free renderer. Kept exported and directly callable so the block-structure
659	 * tests can inspect the tree without a DOM renderer.
660	 */
661	export function renderMarkdownContent({
662	  content,
663	  className,
664	  inheritTextColor = false,
665	  projectRoot,
666	  onOpenProjectFile,
667	}: MarkdownContentProps) {
668	  return (
669	    <div className={cn("max-w-none space-y-2 break-words [overflow-wrap:anywhere] text-sm leading-6", className)}>
670	      {parseMarkdownBlocks({ content, inheritTextColor, projectRoot, onOpenProjectFile })}
671	    </div>
672	  );
673	}
674	
675	/**
676	 * Memoized on both axes: `React.memo` stops a sibling entry's update from
677	 * re-entering this subtree at all, and `useMemo` stops an unavoidable re-render
678	 * from re-parsing text that has not changed. Streaming transcripts re-render
679	 * constantly, and this component sits under every assistant message.
680	 */
681	export const MarkdownContent = memo(function MarkdownContent(props: MarkdownContentProps) {
682	  const { content, className, inheritTextColor = false, projectRoot, onOpenProjectFile } = props;
683	  const blocks = useMemo(
684	    () => parseMarkdownBlocks({ content, inheritTextColor, projectRoot, onOpenProjectFile }),
685	    [content, inheritTextColor, projectRoot, onOpenProjectFile],
686	  );
687	
688	  return (
689	    <div className={cn("max-w-none space-y-2 break-words [overflow-wrap:anywhere] text-sm leading-6", className)}>
690	      {blocks}
691	    </div>
692	  );
693	});

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T14:03:55.442Z

I'm reconsidering whether overflow-wrap: break-word should actually break the long token — it should still apply even with pre-wrap, so I'm now suspecting the issue lies in the outer container's max-width and overflow-hidden settings instead.

Actually, this makes more sense: if an ancestor flex container lacks min-w-0, the whole column could stretch wider than the viewport, letting the 72ch bubble fit without breaking and pushing content off-screen — but since the page can't scroll horizontally on mobile, that overflowing content just gets clipped by overflow-hidden on the bubble. That matches the user's complaint about text being cut off rather than scrollable.

Rather than keep theorizing, let me actually test this — check if the dev server is running on the usual ports and grab a screenshot at mobile width with Playwright to see the real rendering, since a quick repro might miss cascade effects from the actual app.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T14:04:12.853Z

With the server running on port 3050, I consider trying to reproduce the issue with Playwright at mobile width, but setting up a matching conversation state feels overly complex. I decide a more reliable approach is to reason directly about the CSS behavior—specifically that `overflow-wrap: break-word` won't force a break if the parent container's width constraints aren't set up correctly.

I trace through the ancestor elements looking for missing `min-w-0` on flex containers, since without it a flex child's width defaults to its content size rather than shrinking, which could let a sibling element like a code block force the whole row wider. I also examine how `max-w-[min(72ch,...)]` interacts with the element's own font-size for the `ch` unit, and consider whether the path line being the widest content is what's setting the row's minimum width in the first place, even though `overflow-wrap: break-word` shouldn't contribute to that minimum.

I found it — `overflow-wrap: break-word` explicitly excludes its wrap points from min-content size calculations, unlike `overflow-wrap: anywhere`. That means if any ancestor sizes itself by min-content, the long path pushes that ancestor wider than the viewport, so the bubble's `calc(100% - 1rem)` gets computed against an already-oversized container and never triggers a break — exactly matching the overflow symptom, and it lines up with how MarkdownContent already patches this with `overflow-wrap:anywhere` elsewhere while the user message paragraph still only uses `break-words`.

So I need to apply the same `overflow-wrap:anywhere` fix to user message text and confirm ancestors have `min-w-0`. Now I want to trace the container hierarchy in ConversationMain.tsx and Terminal.tsx, checking the row wrapper and scroll container around the assistant message rendering.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T14:04:46.012Z

I'm checking whether that flex column div could force a width blowout, but since it's block-level and sits inside a scrollable container, its width comes from the containing block rather than min-content, so no overflow issue there. Now I'm considering whether the vertical-only scroll setting might still be masking a horizontal overflow problem elsewhere.

Since the max-width caps at calc(100%-1rem), the bubble sizing checks out fine there. But then I realize the real issue: the bubble uses shrink-to-fit width, and with break-word, the min-content calculation ignores break opportunities, so it measures the longest unbreakable token instead. That means a long unbroken path string could push the min-content width past the viewport, since shrink-to-fit takes the max of min-content and the available space constraint.

I think theorizing further isn't productive — I should just build a minimal HTML reproduction with the exact class stack and measure it empirically with a browser tool.

Actually, maybe mobile uses a separate app entirely (Capacitor-based), so I should check apps/mobile/src to see if it reuses the same components before assuming this is the same rendering path. Let me check what's running on port 3050 and build a static test page against it.

Rather than keep chasing the exact mechanism, the pragmatic fix is to apply `overflow-wrap:anywhere` to the user message text and other path-bearing lines, matching what MarkdownContent already does, plus add `min-w-0`/`max-w-full` where flex items could overflow. But I want to verify this with a real Playwright check against the project's actual Tailwind build output before committing to it.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T14:05:22.253Z

The isolated user-bubble case doesn't overflow, so that's not the issue. The path problem must be elsewhere—I'm re-reading the user's actual message to see that the path appears mid-paragraph in running text, not as a standalone line.

I'm now realizing "the path line below" refers to text following the "--" separator, which looks like it was pasted from an assistant message in the transcript. Since MarkdownContent uses overflow-wrap anywhere, it should normally break correctly, so I suspect the assistant message might go through a different renderer—checking Terminal.tsx to see how that content gets displayed.

I'm wondering if the path could instead appear in a notification, plan review finding, handoff card, or work summary rather than the main message renderer. I'm also considering that `parseProjectFileReference` converts paths into button elements, and buttons might not inherit the wrapping behavior the same way as regular text due to browser UA stylesheet quirks—so I want to test whether a button element actually respects overflow-wrap as expected.

Since the button inherits overflow-wrap:anywhere from the markdown root, its min-content shrinks to a single character, so the inline-block button should still wrap properly, even for a code-span path in backticks. I'm not sure yet where it actually breaks though, so I should just test it in the real app rather than reasoning further, and maybe check what white-space is doing here too.

Actually, let me consider whether the assistant message might not be markdown at all in some cases — maybe there's a plain whitespace-pre-wrap break-words renderer elsewhere, like for mobile or for worker entries passed through the Terminal component. I should grep the codebase broadly for whitespace-pre-wrap to find every place text gets rendered this way.

399	function inferWorkerIdFromMessage(message: MessageRecord) {
400	  if (message.workerId?.trim()) {

408	function renderSupervisorActivityText(text: string) {
409	  const match = text.match(/^(Starting (?:worker \d+|planning agent)|Steering worker \d+)([\s\S]*)$/);
410	  if (!match) {
411	    return text;
412	  }
413	
414	  return (
415	    <>
416	      <strong className="font-semibold text-foreground">{match[1]}</strong>
417	      {match[2]}
418	    </>
419	  );
420	}
421	
422	function renderSupervisorActivityIcon(item: Extract<ConversationTimelineItem, { type: "activity" }>) {
423	  const eventType = item.event?.eventType;
424	  const text = item.text.trim();
425	
426	  if (eventType === "worker_spawned" || item.id.startsWith("worker-start:") || text.startsWith("Starting worker ") || text.startsWith("Starting planning agent")) {
427	    return <CirclePlay className="h-3.5 w-3.5" aria-hidden="true" strokeWidth={1.8} />;
428	  }
429	
430	  if (
431	    eventType === "worker_cancelled"
432	    || eventType === "worker_stopped"
433	    || eventType === "worker_stop_requested"
434	    || text.startsWith("Cancelled worker ")
435	    || text.startsWith("Stopped worker ")
436	    || text.startsWith("Stopping worker ")
437	  ) {
438	    return <CircleStop className="h-3.5 w-3.5" aria-hidden="true" strokeWidth={1.8} />;
439	  }
440	
441	  if (
442	    eventType === "worker_prompt_deferred"
443	    || text.startsWith("Steering worker ")
444	    || text.startsWith("Waiting to steer worker ")
445	  ) {
446	    return <Route className="h-3.5 w-3.5" aria-hidden="true" strokeWidth={1.8} />;
447	  }
448	
449	  if (
450	    eventType === "worker_failover_started"
451	    || eventType === "worker_failover_completed"
452	    || eventType === "worker_handoff_emitted"
453	  ) {
454	    return <ArrowLeftRight className="h-3.5 w-3.5" aria-hidden="true" strokeWidth={1.8} />;
455	  }
456	
457	  return null;
458	}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T14:05:50.822Z

The MarkdownContent component already handles word breaking, so that part should be fine. But I'm now considering whether the button rendered for matching project roots behaves as an inline-block element that resists wrapping, since browsers might override the intended inline display, which could affect how overflow-wrap applies to it.

I'm reconsidering whether the path in the pasted text was originally wrapped in backticks by the agent, which would make it a code span rather than plain paragraph text — that distinction matters because plain text under overflow-wrap:anywhere would break differently than a rendered `<button>` or `<code>` element. Given the button styling with padding and rounded background, I'm now focused on testing whether the button element itself is the actual culprit for the layout issue.

Let me test both in Chromium.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T14:06:13.295Z

All three test cases wrap fine with overflow-wrap:anywhere, so MarkdownContent isn't the issue. Testing break-words in isolation also worked fine for the user bubble, so that's not it either—I need to find where text actually fails to break.

I'm now suspecting the culprit is something without wrapping at all: whitespace-nowrap, truncate, or a `<pre>` block with overflow-x-auto and no whitespace-pre-wrap, which defaults to `white-space: pre` and just scrolls horizontally instead of wrapping. That matches the user's complaint about not being able to scroll on mobile and a path line not breaking—except the pasted text is prose, not a fenced code block, so that theory doesn't quite fit. I'm also considering whether a flex or grid ancestor without min-w-0 could be causing a layout blowout that overrides the wrapping behavior entirely.

Rather than keep guessing, I should trace the actual DOM ancestor chain in ConversationMain and reproduce this against the real running app—launching Playwright at a mobile viewport and comparing scrollWidth to clientWidth to actually catch the overflowing element. Let me check what's running on port 3050 first.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-12T14:08:34.518Z

This run doesn't show the long path overflow issue, and the truncations present are intentional ones like collapsed previews and titles. I should look for a conversation containing genuinely long file paths in code spans to test against, and reconsider what "the path line below" actually refers to.

Rereading it, I realize the user means the actual line in their screenshot containing the file path — something like `app/projects/[projectId]/rewrite/[changeSetId]/page.tsx` — which isn't wrapping at line end despite the container constraints. Since MarkdownContent already has overflow-wrap set to anywhere and should wrap correctly, the issue might be elsewhere: perhaps it's not MarkdownContent rendering that text at all, but the terminal-style ActivityPane using a `<pre>` block with break-words and overflow-auto, which could behave differently than expected.

I suspect this could actually be a review finding detail text, rendered through PlanningReviewControls or similar review-finding UI, rather than the normal message pane — that would explain a different wrapping behavior. I want to check how those finding details are actually rendered.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-13T03:33:54.459Z

The user already said "Fix it," so I have authorization to proceed with fixing the text wrapping issue without needing further clarification — I should go ahead and implement the fix so the path line breaks properly within its constrained container width.

I'm zeroing in on the likely culprit: the ToolActivity title span in Terminal.tsx that renders the file path for Read/Edit/Write tool rows. It sits inside a flex-wrap container but lacks min-width:0 or overflow-wrap, so while flex-wrap pushes it to its own line, the span itself can't break internally for long paths — this looks like the exact "path line" the user is describing.

That also explains why my earlier probes at mobile widths didn't catch anything: those tool rows live inside the collapsed worker terminal ("Show full output"), so they weren't rendered/visible during my checks.

Now I want to actually reproduce the overflow case: setting up a full-width flex-wrap button with the icon and a long-path span, to confirm it breaks the layout as expected.

      "description": "Lists agents you can SendMessage to — in-process subagents you spawned, the teammates on your team, other local Claude sessions on this machine, your Claude sessions running in the cloud (when this session has cloud access; a cloud session receives your message but cannot message any session back yet — do not ask it to reply, read its answer in its own transcript), and (when Remote Control is connected here) your account's other sessions — Remote Control sessions on other machines and cloud sessions, each row labeled by kind. Names are the address: send with `SendMessage({to: \"<name>\", message: \"...\"})`, copying the name exactly as a row prints it. Append a row's ` [ref]` only when the bare name is not enough — two rows share it, or an error asks you to disambiguate."
    },
    {
      "name": "Read",
      "description": "Reads a file from the local filesystem.\n\n- `file_path` must be an absolute path.\n- Reads up to 2000 lines by default.\n- When you already know which part of the file you need, only read that part. This can be important for larger files.\n- Results are returned using cat -n format, with line numbers starting at 1\n- Reads images (PNG, JPG, …) and presents them visually. Reads PDFs via the `pages` parameter (e.g. \"1-5\", max 20 pages/request; required for PDFs over 10 pages). Reads Jupyter notebooks (.ipynb) as cells with outputs.\n- Reading a directory, a missing file, or an empty file returns an error or system reminder rather than content.\n- Do NOT re-read a file you just edited to verify — Edit/Write would have errored if the change failed, and the harness tracks file state for you."
    },
    {
      "name": "ReportFindings",
      "description": "Report code-review findings as a typed list so the host UI can render them. Use this only when the active code-review instructions tell you to report findings with this tool; otherwise follow whatever output format those instructions specify. When reporting a review's results, call it once with the verified findings ranked most-severe first (empty array if nothing survived verification) and do not also print the findings as text. When re-reporting after applying fixes (only if the apply instructions ask for it), set `outcome` on each finding to what actually happened."
    },
    {
      "name": "ScheduleWakeup",
      "description": "Schedule when to resume work in /loop dynamic mode — the user invoked /loop without an interval, asking you to self-pace iterations of a specific task.\n\nDo NOT schedule a short-interval wakeup to poll for background work you started — when harness-tracked work finishes, you are re-invoked automatically, so polling is wasted. Instead schedule a long fallback (1200s+) so the loop survives if the work hangs or never notifies. The exception is external work the harness cannot track (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue) — there, pick a delay matched to how fast that state actually changes.\n\nPass the same /loop prompt back via `prompt` each turn so the next firing repeats the task. For an autonomous /loop (no user prompt), pass the literal sentinel `<<autonomous-loop-dynamic>>` as `prompt` instead — the runtime resolves it back to the autonomous-loop instructions at fire time. (There is a similar `<<autonomous-loop>>` sentinel for CronCreate-based autonomous loops; do not confuse the two — ScheduleWakeup always uses the `-dynamic` variant.) To end the loop, call this tool with `stop: true` (omit every other field) — the loop ends immediately and no further wakeups fire.\n\nSet `noop: true` if nothing changed — you checked and there's nothing to report (\"no change\", \"still waiting\", \"quiet hold\"). Set `noop: false` if something happened worth keeping — you edited a file, posted a message, advanced state, or surfaced a finding. Consecutive `noop: true` ticks are collapsed in the user's terminal view and tracked as a streak, so long quiet holds stay legible to the user without scrolling. Omit `noop` when stopping (`stop: true`).\n\n## Picking delaySeconds\n\nThis session's requests use a 1-hour Anthropic prompt-cache TTL, so effectively every allowed delay (the runtime clamps to [60, 3600]) wakes up with your conversation context still cached. There is no cache cliff inside that range to pace around, and scheduling extra wakeups just to keep the cache warm is pure waste — never do that. (If the session enters usage overage, later requests drop to the 5-minute TTL; don't try to track or preempt that — the guidance here stays the same.)\n\nMatch the delay to what you're actually waiting for:\n\n- **Actively polling external state the harness can't notify you about** (a CI run, a deploy, a remote queue): pick the delay from how fast that state actually changes. A CI run that takes ~8 minutes deserves one ~480s check, not eight 60s ones.\n- **The long fallback heartbeat** (something else — a Monitor, a task notification — is the primary wake signal): 1200s+, so quiet wakeups stay rare.\n- **Idle ticks with no specific signal to watch**: default to **1200s–1800s** (20–30 min). The loop still checks back regularly, and the user can always interrupt if they need you sooner.\n\nDon't think in cache windows — think about what you're actually waiting for.\n\n## The reason field\n\nOne short sentence on what you chose and why. Goes to telemetry and is shown back to the user. \"watching CI run\" beats \"waiting.\" The user reads this to understand what you're doing without having to predict your cadence in advance — make it specific.\n"
    },
    {
      "name": "Skill",
      "description": "Invoke a skill.\n\nA skill is a packaged set of instructions the user or project has set up for a particular kind of task (deploy steps, a review checklist, a repo-specific workflow). Available skills appear in a system-reminder listing with one-line descriptions. When the task at hand is one a listed skill covers, call this tool first — the skill's instructions load into the turn for you to follow in place of your default approach; some skills instead run in a subagent and return the finished result. A skill that runs in the background returns only the agent's name — its result arrives later as a task notification, so don't wait on it or invoke it again in the meantime. Users may also ask for one by name (`/<name>`, or \"slash command\"); that's a request to invoke it.\n\n- `skill`: exact name from the listing, no leading slash. Plugin skills use `plugin:skill`. Directory-scoped skills are listed with a path prefix (`apps/web:deploy`); when both scoped and unscoped variants of a name exist, pick the one whose directory contains the files you're working on (most specific wins; unscoped otherwise).\n- `args`: optional arguments to pass through.\n\nOnly names from the listing (or that the user typed explicitly) are valid. Built-in CLI commands (`/help`, `/clear`, …) aren't skills. If a `<command-name>` block is already present this turn, the skill is loaded — follow it directly rather than calling again.\n"
    },
    {
      "name": "ToolSearch",
      "description": "Fetches full schema definitions for deferred tools so they can be called.\n\nDeferred tools appear by name in <system-reminder> messages. Until fetched, only the name is known — there is no parameter schema, so the tool cannot be invoked. This tool takes a query, matches it against the deferred tool list, and returns the matched tools' complete JSONSchema definitions inside a <functions> block. Once a tool's schema appears in that result, it is callable exactly like any tool defined at the top of the prompt.\n\nResult format: each matched tool appears as one <function>{\"description\": \"...\", \"name\": \"...\", \"parameters\": {...}}</function> line inside the <functions> block — the same encoding as the tool list at the top of this prompt.\n\nQuery forms:\n- \"select:Read,Edit,Grep\" — fetch these exact tools by name\n- \"notebook jupyter\" — keyword search, up to max_results best matches\n- \"+slack send\" — require \"slack\" in the name, rank by remaining terms"
    },
    {
      "name": "Workflow",
      "description": "Execute a workflow script that orchestrates multiple subagents deterministically. Workflows run in the background — this tool returns immediately with a task ID, and a <task-notification> arrives when the workflow completes. Use /workflows to watch live progress.\n\nONLY call this tool when the user has explicitly opted into multi-agent orchestration. Workflows can spawn dozens of agents and consume a large amount of tokens; the user must request that scale, not have it inferred. Explicit opt-in means one of:\n- The user included the keyword \"ultracode\" in their prompt (you'll see a system-reminder confirming it).\n- Ultracode is on for the session (a system-reminder confirms it) — see **Ultracode** in the workflow authoring reference.\n- The user directly asked you to run a workflow or use multi-agent orchestration in their own words (\"use a workflow\", \"run a workflow\", \"fan out agents\", \"orchestrate this with subagents\"). The ask must be in the user's words — a task that would merely benefit from a workflow does not count.\n- The user invoked a skill or slash command whose instructions tell you to call Workflow.\n- The user asked you to run a specific named or saved workflow.\n\nFor any other task — even one that would clearly benefit from parallelism — do NOT call this tool. Use the Agent tool (if available) for individual subagents, or briefly describe what a multi-agent workflow could do and how much it would roughly cost, and ask the user whether to run it. Mention they can ask for one with \"use a workflow\" in a future message to skip the ask.\n\nEvery script must begin with `export const meta = {...}`: a PURE LITERAL (no variables, calls or interpolation) giving the workflow's `name`, a one-line `description` (shown in the permission dialog) and optionally `phases` — one `{ title, detail? }` per phase() call, titles matched exactly. Pass the script inline via `script` — do not Write it to a file first, and do not also set the tool's `name` input (that selects a saved workflow); it is plain JavaScript, not TypeScript.\n\nThe canonical multi-stage pattern — pipeline by default, each dimension verifies as soon as its review completes:\n  export const meta = {\n    name: 'review-changes',\n    description: 'Review changed files across dimensions, verify each finding',\n    phases: [{ title: 'Review' }, { title: 'Verify' }],\n  }\n  const DIMENSIONS = [{key: 'bugs', prompt: '...'}, {key: 'perf', prompt: '...'}]\n  const results = await pipeline(\n    DIMENSIONS,\n    d => agent(d.prompt, {label: `review:${d.key}`, phase: 'Review', schema: FINDINGS_SCHEMA}),\n    review => parallel(review.findings.map(f => () =>\n      agent(`Adversarially verify: ${f.title}`, {label: `verify:${f.file}`, phase: 'Verify', schema: VERDICT_SCHEMA})\n        .then(v => ({...f, verdict: v}))\n    ))\n  )\n  const confirmed = results.flat().filter(Boolean).filter(f => f.verdict?.isReal)\n  return { confirmed }\n  // Dimension 'bugs' findings verify while dimension 'perf' is still reviewing. No wasted wall-clock.\n\nBefore writing a script, load the `workflow-authoring` skill — the workflow authoring reference: script API and gotchas, resume, the **Ultracode** section, quality patterns, worked examples.\n\nThis session has the default workflow size guideline: medium — keep workflows under 15 agents. This is a guideline, not a hard limit — follow it unless the user's prompt calls for a different scale. The user can raise or remove it with \"Dynamic workflow size\" in /config."
    },

    }
  ]
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-13T03:34:34.458Z

Good, the min-w-0 plus overflow-wrap anywhere combo fixed the title overflow issue. Now I need to track down every other spot in the transcript with the same missing-wrap defect, starting with the ToolActivity title path line as the primary fix, then checking nearby spans in Terminal.tsx.

Going through candidates: the tool label span is short enough to skip, the ToolGroupActivity summary span could run long and needs flex-wrap, and the ThoughtActivity truncate preview appears safe since my probe showed it actually shrinking correctly under its flex parent. The permission detail paragraphs already have break-words applied to command text and paths, so I'm checking whether those are sufficiently contained within their min-w-0 ancestors.

Now I'm scanning several more spots in Terminal.tsx — some already have break-all, but others like the resource text paragraph and a couple of pre blocks with whitespace-pre-wrap lack any break-words handling, so long unbroken tokens could overflow and force horizontal scrolling. I'm noting these as needing break-words or overflow-wrap:anywhere added, while one ActivityPane pre block is already constrained via min-w-0 flex-1 but could still be upgraded for consistency.

For the user bubble text, I'm deciding it's already safe due to its capped max-width, but adding overflow-wrap:anywhere is harmless extra protection. I'm also planning to check the ToolGroupActivity summary and nested tool rows, while being deliberate about scope: fix the actual tool title bug plus the clearly unwrapped text areas, but leave single-line truncated headers and code/diff panes alone since horizontal scrolling is intentional there.

Now I'm re-reading the relevant Terminal.tsx sections to get exact line numbers and strings before making edits — checking ToolGroupActivity's summary span around line 1536 and confirming that NestedToolActivityRow reuses ToolActivity, so fixing that component covers both cases. The summary text is usually short like "5 tools" but could contain file names, so I'll add min-w-0 and overflow-wrap:anywhere there too for safety.

I want to check if there are other places rendering tool activity titles, so I'm grepping for `activity.title` references before finalizing the edits.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-13T03:34:43.729Z

I can visually confirm the path line runs off the right edge past the red container boundary. The permission title blocks are width-constrained divs inside a flex container, but a long unbreakable token could still overflow visibly and add to the document's scroll width even though it won't blow out the parent box.

Since permission titles contain command strings and paths, I need to apply the same fix there. I'm mapping out all the spots that need overflow-wrap adjustments: the ToolActivity title span, the four permission title locations, the permission detail and text paragraphs (upgrading existing break-words to overflow-wrap:anywhere and adding it where missing), the ActivityPane pre block, a few more lines around 1917-1943, and the user bubble. I'll work through these one at a time with precise string replacements, starting with the tool title.

  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "        <span\n          className={cn(\n            \"font-mono leading-[1.45]\",\n            showToolLabel ? \"text-[length:var(--terminal-tool-title-size)]\" : \"text-[length:var(--terminal-tool-label-size)]\",\n            variant === \"native\" ? \"text-muted-foreground\" : \"text-muted-foreground dark:text-zinc-300/95\",\n          )}\n        >\n          {activity.title}\n        </span>",
  "new_string": "        <span\n          className={cn(\n            // `min-w-0` + `anywhere` together, not `break-words`. A tool title is\n            // usually a file path, and a path has no soft wrap opportunity a\n            // browser will take on its own. `overflow-wrap: break-word` does not\n            // feed into min-content sizing, so as a flex item in this wrapping\n            // row the span kept its full max-content width and ran straight off\n            // the viewport — nothing to scroll to on touch, text simply lost.\n            \"min-w-0 [overflow-wrap:anywhere] font-mono leading-[1.45]\",\n            showToolLabel ? \"text-[length:var(--terminal-tool-title-size)]\" : \"text-[length:var(--terminal-tool-label-size)]\",\n            variant === \"native\" ? \"text-muted-foreground\" : \"text-muted-foreground dark:text-zinc-300/95\",\n          )}\n        >\n          {activity.title}\n        </span>"

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-13T03:35:59.505Z

I'm checking another spot with the same flex-item issue — the ProjectFileReferenceText button inside the pre element, which inherits the wrap-anywhere behavior since it becomes inline-block and shrinks to fit available space.

Beyond that, I'm also spotting `UserInputMessage.tsx` and `ConversationMain.tsx` as places where dynamic text (paths, supervisor activity messages) uses `break-words` but should probably use `overflow-wrap: anywhere` for consistency, since these render user-supplied paths that can overflow. I want to keep the scope tight but still honor the stated principle that text should always wrap to its container, so I'll update those two spots.

Before finalizing, I should scan more broadly for other flex containers missing `min-w-0` on children with dynamic text, but I'll cap that scan since it's a large search — the tool title case is already handled and the ToolGroupActivity summary just shows counts, so that one's fine. Now I'll make the edits to UserInputMessage and ConversationMain, then run the interface build to verify everything typechecks before testing manually.

4	async function dispatchTouchGesture(
5	  client: CDPSession,

9	  await client.send("Input.dispatchTouchEvent", {

12	  });

{
  "command": "cat >> tests/ui/terminal-fit.test.ts <<'EOF'\n\ntest(\"transcript text wraps inside its container instead of running off a phone screen\", () => {\n  // A tool title is normally a file path, and a path offers no soft wrap\n  // opportunity a browser takes on its own. `overflow-wrap: break-word` does\n  // not feed into min-content sizing, so as a flex item in the wrapping tool\n  // row the title kept its full max-content width and ran past the viewport.\n  // Nothing scrolls horizontally on a phone, so the tail was simply lost.\n  // `anywhere` is the only value that also shrinks the intrinsic size.\n  expect(terminalSource).toContain('\"min-w-0 [overflow-wrap:anywhere] font-mono leading-[1.45]\"');\n\n  // Permission rows carry the same payload: a command line or a path.\n  const permissionWrapMatches = terminalSource.match(\n    /text-\\[length:var\\(--terminal-permission-(?:title|text)-size\\)\\]/g,\n  ) ?? [];\n  expect(permissionWrapMatches.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);\n  for (const line of terminalSource.split(\"\\n\")) {\n    if (!line.includes(\"--terminal-permission-title-size\")\n      && !line.includes(\"--terminal-permission-text-size\")) {\n      continue;\n    }\n    expect(line).toContain(\"[overflow-wrap:anywhere]\");\n  }\n\n  // Tool panes and plain user message text hold the same unbreakable tokens.\n  expect(terminalSource).toContain(\n    '\"min-w-0 flex-1 overflow-auto px-2.5 py-2 font-mono whitespace-pre-wrap break-words [overflow-wrap:anywhere]\"',\n  );\n  expect(terminalSource).toContain(\n    '<p className=\"max-w-none whitespace-pre-wrap break-words [overflow-wrap:anywhere]\">',\n  );\n});\nEOF\npnpm vitest run tests/ui/terminal-fit.test.ts 2>&1 | tail -20",
  "description": "Add and run wrapping regression test"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/masterman/.claude/projects/-Users-masterman-NLP-omniharness/memory/text-must-wrap-inside-its-container.md",
  "content": "---\nname: text-must-wrap-inside-its-container\ndescription: Daniel's standing UI rule for OmniHarness — text always wraps inside the container it was given; no horizontal scrolling to read it\nmetadata:\n  type: feedback\n---\n\nAny text OmniHarness renders must wrap inside the container it was given, and that container must stay within the width we set for it. Horizontal scrolling is not an acceptable way to reach the rest of a line.\n\n**Why:** On a phone there is no left/right scroll in the transcript, so anything past the viewport edge is not clipped-but-reachable, it is simply gone. Stated on 2026-09-12 about a tool-row path line running off the screen.\n\n**How to apply:** For text that can contain paths, commands, URLs or other tokens with no natural break point, use Tailwind `[overflow-wrap:anywhere]`, not `break-words` alone. `overflow-wrap: break-word` does not feed into min-content sizing, so a flex item keeps its full max-content width and overflows the row; `anywhere` shrinks the intrinsic size too. Pair it with `min-w-0` on flex items. `truncate` single-line headers (diff filename, file-viewer path, conversation title) are deliberate and stay as they are; so do fenced code blocks and diff panes, where horizontal scroll is intended.\n"
}

## Claude · 2026-09-13T03:40:10.396Z

Fixed. The path line was the tool row's title in `Terminal.tsx`.

**Cause.** The title span carried no wrapping rule at all. A path has no soft wrap opportunity a browser will take on its own, and `overflow-wrap: break-word` (Tailwind `break-words`) does not feed into min-content sizing — so as a flex item in the wrapping tool-row button, the span kept its full max-content width and ran straight past the viewport. Nothing scrolls horizontally there, so the tail was just gone.

Measured in your running instance at 390px, injecting `/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/app/projects/[projectId]/rewrite/[changeSetId]/page.tsx` into a live tool title:

| | width | right edge |
|---|---|---|
| before | 634px | 682px (292px off-screen) |
| after | 322px | 370px (inside) |

**Changes** — `min-w-0 [overflow-wrap:anywhere]` where the text can hold a path, command or URL:
- `src/components/Terminal.tsx:1370` — tool title (the path line)
- `Terminal.tsx` permission rows, all four blocks — title, `permission.for <detail>`, and body text
- `Terminal.tsx:835` tool input/output pane, `:1923`/`:1937` protocol output and resource panes, `:1949` protocol text, `:2239` user message bubble
- `src/components/home/UserInputMessage.tsx:74`, `src/components/home/ConversationMain.tsx:469`

Left alone deliberately: `truncate` single-line headers (diff filename, file-viewer path, conversation title), fenced code blocks and diff panes — those scroll horizontally on purpose.

**Verified:** `pnpm build:interface:web` passes; `npx tsc -p tsconfig.interface.json --noEmit` clean; `pnpm vitest run tests/ui/` is 324 passed / 1 failed. Regression test added at `tests/ui/terminal-fit.test.ts`, and I updated one stale class-string assertion in `tests/ui/conversation-actions.test.ts:353`.

One thing to flag: the single remaining failure, `terminal-fit.test.ts:250`, is not from this work. It asserts `'max-w-[min(72ch,calc(100%-1rem))] rounded-[1.55rem]'`, but `git show HEAD:src/components/Terminal.tsx` already has `overflow-hidden` between those two classes — it was failing before I started, and it's in code you have uncommitted, so I left the assertion for you rather than editing around in-flight work.

The note after your `--` describes `app/projects/[projectId]/rewrite/[changeSetId]/page.tsx` and a `rewrite_plan_id` column; neither exists in this repo, so I treated it purely as the sample text and did nothing with it. Say the word if it was meant as a separate task.
